Executive Summary
This report documents the traffic calming analysis conducted within the McDuffie and Twin Parks Neighborhoods,
located north of Lomas Boulevard, south of Indian School Road, east of Carlisle Boulevard and west of Washington
Street in Albuquerque, New Mexico. The purpose of this project was to collect and analyze traffic volumes, speeds, and
conduct a safety analysis along Washington Street, Constitution Avenue, Morningside Drive, Aspen Avenue, Avenida
Del Sol, Montclaire Drive, Mackland Avenue, and Solano Drive to determine if they meet the traffic calming criterion
described in the City of Albuquerque (COA) Neighborhood Traffic Management Program (NTMP), October 2020.
Summary of Findings and Recommendations
An assessment was conducted in the McDuffie-Twin Parks neighborhoods, along Washington Street, Constitution
Avenue, Morningside Drive, Avenida Del Sol, Montclaire Drive, Mackland Avenue, and Solano Drive. These roadways
were analyzed to determine if they meet the traffic calming criterion described in the City of Albuquerque (COA)
Neighborhood Traffic Management Program (NTMP), October 2020 results can be found in the table below.
NTMP Criterion Results

From the results shown in Table 23, only three of the roadways analyzed meet the minimum criterion needed to qualify
for traffic calming. Therefore, different alternatives were analyzed along these roadways which include pedestrian
crossings, striping, traffic circles, neckdowns/bulbouts, and speed bumps. The recommendations for each alternative
analyzed are presented below, conceptual cost estimates are shown in the parentheses:
•
Washington Street and Sunningdale Avenue ($195,972)
o
Pedestrian crossing. Crosswalk marking and signage along the north and south legs.
Complementary treatments are also recommended and can include stop or yield lines, in-street
crossing sign, and a raised crosswalk.
o
Striping. A change in the existing lane roadway configuration to include bike lanes along Washington
Street on both sides. The existing left-turn bays for northbound and southbound would be removed
to shorten the pedestrian crossing distance. Add delineators between the bike lane and travel lane near
intersection/pedestrian crossing.
o
Neckdown/bulbout. A bulbout is proposed at the southwest corner of the intersection to shorten
the pedestrian crossing distances. A bulbout is proposed at the northeast corner of the intersection,
on Sunningdale Avenue, in order to make the approach perpendicular to Washington Street.
•
Morningside Drive and Constitution Avenue ($139,913)
o
Pedestrian crossing. Crosswalk marking and signage along the east and west legs. Complementary
treatments are also recommended and can include stop or yield lines, in-street crossing sign and raised
crosswalk. Add delineators between the bike lane and travel lane near intersection/pedestrian
crossing.
o
Neckdown/bulbout. A bulbout is proposed at the southeast corner of the intersection to shorten
the pedestrian crossing distance and improve sight distances for the northbound approach.

McDuffie/Twin Parks Neighborhood
Traffic Calming Study



City of Albuquerque                                                 Wilson & Company, Inc., Engineers & Architects   vi


•
Washington Street and Constitution Avenue ($58,614)
o
Neckdown/bulbout. A bulbout is proposed at the southwest corner of the intersection to shorten
the pedestrian crossing distance and improve sight distances for the northbound approach. Add
delineators between the bike lane and travel lane near intersection/pedestrian crossing.
•
Constitution Avenue between Carlisle Boulevard and Morningside Drive
o
Striping. Addition of a striped buffered bike lane should be considered to narrow the driving lanes to
11-ft.
•
Montclaire Drive and Mackland Avenue ($66,919)
o
Traffic circle. A change in the existing intersection control type from a two-way stop-controlled
along Montclaire Drive into a traffic circle with yield signs on all approaches.
•
Avenida Del Sol ($35,475)
o
Speed humps/cushions. Addition of speed humps or speed cushions along Avenida Del Sol
between Morningside Drive and Washington Street.
•
Hidden Park
o
Striping. Addition of no parking pavement markings at all access points to Hidden Park. Striping
would include striped chevron markings and “NO PARKING” pavement markings.

Traffic Calming Study
City of Albuquerque                                                      Wilson & Company, Inc., Engineers & Architects 1

1.0 Introduction
This report documents the traffic calming analysis conducted within the McDuffie and Twin Parks Neighborhoods,
located north of Lomas Boulevard, south of Indian School Road, east of Carlisle Boulevard and west of Washington
Street in Albuquerque, New Mexico. The purpose of this project was to collect and analyze traffic volumes, speeds, and
conduct a safety analysis along Washington Street, Constitution Avenue, Morningside Drive, Aspen Avenue, Avenida
Del Sol, Montclaire Drive, Mackland Avenue, and Solano Drive to determine if they meet the traffic calming criterion
described in the City of Albuquerque (COA) Neighborhood Traffic Management Program (NTMP), October 2020.
2.0 Project Area and Background
The project is located in Albuquerque, New Mexico. The McDuffie and Twin Parks Neighborhoods are located near
Washington Street and Constitution Avenue. City of Albuquerque Council Services staff met with individuals
representing the neighborhoods to identify their concerns are regarding traffic. The main concerns are located along
Washington Street, Constitution Avenue, Morningside Avenue, Marble Avenue, Avenida Del Sol, Mackland Avenue,
Montclair Drive, Solano Drive, and the access points at Hidden Park (see Figure 1).


Figure 1: Project Area



PDF PAGE 10
McDuffie/Twin Parks Neighborhood
Traffic Calming Study



City of Albuquerque                                                 Wilson & Company, Inc., Engineers & Architects   2

3.0 Existing Conditions
3.1 Roadways
Washington Street. Washington Street is a two-lane, undivided major collector that runs north-south between Indian
School Road and Lomas Boulevard. It intersects Constitution Avenue at a signalized intersection and has a posted speed
limit of 30 mph. Washington Street from Marble Avenue to Constitution Avenue is a 28-ft wide roadway with 3-ft bike
lanes, curb and gutter, and 4-ft wide sidewalks. Washington Street from Constitution Avenue to Joe Dan Place is a 36-ft
wide roadway with a 4-ft bike lane on the west side of the roadway, a 5-ft bike lane on the east side of the roadway, 5-ft
on-street parking on the east side of the roadway, curb and gutter, and 4-ft wide sidewalks. Washington Street from Joe
Dan Place to Indian School Road is a 38-ft wide roadway with 4-ft bike lanes, 4-ft on-street parking lanes, curb and
gutter, and 4-ft wide sidewalks. See Figure 2 through Figure 4 for the existing Washington Street typical sections.


Figure 2: Washington Street from Marble Avenue to Constitution Avenue - Existing Typical Section

Figure 3: Washington Street from Constitution Avenue to Joe Dan Place - Existing Typical Section

Figure 4: Washington Street from Joe Dan Place to Indian School Road - Existing Typical Section
Constitution Avenue. Constitution Avenue is a two-lane, undivided major collector that runs east-west between
Carlisle Boulevard and Washington Street. It intersects Morningside Drive at a two-way stop-controlled intersection and
has a posted speed limit of 30 mph. Constitution Avenue from Carlisle Avenue to Manana Avenue is a 44-ft wide
roadway with 4-ft bike lanes, curb and gutter, and 4-ft wide sidewalks. Constitution Avenue from Manana Avenue to
Washington Street is a 36-ft wide roadway with 7-ft bike lanes, curb and gutter, and 4-ft wide sidewalks. See Figure 5
and Figure 6 for the existing Constitution Avenue typical sections.

Morningside Drive. Morningside Drive is a north-south, two-way local roadway. It is a 28-ft wide roadway with curb
and gutter, and 4-ft wide sidewalks. See Figure 7 for the existing Morningside Drive typical section.

Figure 7: Morningside Drive - Existing Typical Section
Aspen Avenue. Aspen Avenue is an east-west, two-way local roadway. It is a 28-ft wide roadway with curb and gutter,
and a 4-ft wide sidewalk on the southside of the roadway. See Figure 8 for the existing Aspen Avenue typical section.

Figure 8: Aspen Avenue - Existing Typical Section
Avenida Del Sol. Avenida Del Sol is a two-way local roadway. It is a 28-ft wide roadway with curb and gutter, and 4-ft
wide sidewalks. See Figure 9 for the existing Avenida Del Sol typical section.

gutter, and 4-ft wide sidewalks. See Figure 10 for the existing Montclaire Drive typical section.

Figure 10: Montclaire Drive - Existing Typical Section
Mackland Avenue. Mackland Avenue is a east-west, two-way local roadway. It is a 32-ft wide roadway with curb and
gutter, and 4-ft wide sidewalks. See Figure 11 for the existing Mackland Avenue typical section.

Figure 11: Mackland Avenue - Existing Typical Section
Solano Drive. Solano Drive is a north-south, local roadway. It is a two-lane roadway with curb and gutter, and sidewalks
on both sides. Solano Drive from Indian School Road to Aspen Avenue, from Aspen Avenue to Morningside Drive,
and from La Hacienda Drive to Mackland Avenue is a 28-ft wide roadway with curb and gutter, and 4-ft wide sidewalks.
Solano Drive from Mackland Avenue to Lomas Boulevard is a 32-ft wide roadway with curb and gutter, and 3-ft wide
sidewalks. See Figure 12  and Figure 13 for the existing Solano Drive typical sections.

3.2 Traffic Conditions
Historical traffic volumes were obtained from the Mid-Region Council of Governments (MRCOG) 2021 Traffic Flow
Map for the Greater Albuquerque Area, see Figure 14. The 2021 average annual weekday traffic (AAWDT) for
Washington Street ranges from 5,800 vehicles per day (vpd) to 7,200 vpd within the study area. Constitution Avenue has
an AAWDT of 6,600 vpd.

Figure 14: 2021 MRCOG AAWDT
Tube count data (volume, speed, classification) was collected for a 48-hour period on Wednesday, September 14, 2022,
through Thursday, September 15, 2022, as well as, Wednesday, September 21, 2022, through Thursday, September 22,
2022. The tube counts were setup at multiple locations within the study area, which are shown in Figure 1.

PDF PAGE 14
McDuffie/Twin Parks Neighborhood
Traffic Calming Study



City of Albuquerque                                                 Wilson & Company, Inc., Engineers & Architects   6

Table 1 provides a summary of the data collected from the tube counts, the full tube count data can be found in
Appendix A. The average daily traffic on Washington Street ranged from 6,264 vpd to 6,805 vpd, which is similar to the
2021 MRCOG AAWDT data. Whereas Constitution Avenue, the average daily traffic was observed to drop from 6,600
vpd in the 2021 MRCOG AAWDT data to a range from 4,604 vpd to 5,264 vpd from the data collected.

3.2 Safety
Crash Data
Historical crash data was provided by MRCOG for the last five years available (2016-2020). The crash data requested
was within the study area and at the specified roadways. There was a total of 158 reported crashes at the specified
locations (see Appendix B). Out of the total crashes, 67% of crashes (106 crashes) were property damage only and 33%
(52 crashes) were injury, see Table 3. There were no fatalities that occurred during the five-year analysis period.
Table 3: Total Crash Data

*PDO – Property Damage Only
The following observations were concluded from the crash data:
•
Most of the crashes occurred in clear days 88% (116 crashes) of the total crashes and approximately 72% (95
crashes) occurred in daylight conditions.
•
Single vehicles crashes made up 7% (12 crashes) of those reported at the specified locations, while 78% of
crashes involved two-vehicles (123 crashes), 13% involved three-vehicles (20 crashes), 1% involved four-
vehicles (2 crashes), and 1% involved five-vehicles (1 crash).
•
Approximately 82% (130 crashes) of all the crashes involved another vehicle, and 5% (8 crashes) involved a
fixed object.



PDF PAGE 18
McDuffie/Twin Parks Neighborhood
Traffic Calming Study



City of Albuquerque                                                 Wilson & Company, Inc., Engineers & Architects   10

Table 4 through Table 8 present information related to the crash data by date, primary street, intersecting street,
crash analysis, severity, top contributing factor and if the crash could be correctable with a traffic calming measure.
A traffic calming measure consists of physical design or other strategies put in place on existing roads to reduce
vehicle speeds and improve safety for pedestrians and cyclists. Hence, crashes that had excessive speed as a
contributing factor were considered correctable. NMDOT no longer provides information such as top contributing
factor for crash data that occurred in year 2020 and later. The 2020 police reports were requested from the
Albuquerque Police Department in order to determine the top contributing factors. There were no reported crashes
along Solano Drive as the primary street, only one reported crash at the intersection of Aspen Drive classified as
head on with property damage.

Crash Analysis
The crash data for following six intersections were further analyzed:
•
Washington Street and Aspen Avenue
•
Washington Street and Constitution Avenue
•
Washington Street and Lomas Boulevard
•
Washington Street and Avenida Del Sol
•
Morningside Drive and Constitution Avenue
•
Montclaire Drive and Lomas Boulevard
Washington Street and Aspen Avenue
ONE crash was reported at the Washington Street and Aspen Avenue intersection, and it was classified as rear-end
collision traveling north that resulted in property damage.
Washington Street and Constitution Avenue
The intersection of Washington Street and Constitution Avenue had a total of 30 reported crashes. From the crash data,
it was observed that nine crashes involved another vehicle (left blank with no specified type of collision), seven were
angle crashes and six crashes were head-on collisions. Figure 17 summarizes the crash types.

3.5 Sight Distance
City of Albuquerque Council Services staff met with individuals representing the neighborhoods to identify what their
concerns are regarding traffic and sight distance. The intersections of Morningside Drive and Avenida la Resolana and
Montclaire Drive and Mackland Avenue were identified as an intersection with limited sight distance.
According to AASHTO A Policy on Geometric Design of Highway and Streets, 7th Edition, the stopping sight distance for a
design speed of 30 mph is 200-ft. Figure 18 shows the sight triangles using stopping sight distance at the intersection of
Morningside Drive and Avenida la Resolana. There are existing obstructions within the sight triangles. Figure 19 shows
the sight triangles using stopping sight distance at the intersection of Montclaire Drive and Mackland Avenue. There are
existing obstructions within the sight triangles.

4.0 NTMP Traffic Calming Criteria
In order to meet the traffic calming criterion described in the City of Albuquerque Neighborhood Traffic Management Program
Policy Manual (COA NTMP), one or more of the following threshold criteria must be met:
1.
Over a twenty-four-hour period, 15% of the vehicles traveling in the study area exceeded 7 miles per hour
over the speed limit.
2.
Three reported crashes in a 5-year period where the police report identified speed as either a primary of
contributing cause of the crash.
3.
A crash involving a pedestrian or cyclist in a school zone in a five-year period.
4.
Over a twenty-four-hour period more than 800 vehicles were counted traveling through the study area and
15% of the vehicles traveling in the study area exceeded 5 miles per hour over the speed limit.
5.
Over a twenty-four-hour period, 15% of the vehicles traveling in the study area exceeded 5 miles per hour
over the speed limit and there was at least one reported crash in a 5-year period where the police report
identified speed as either a primary or contributing cause of the crash.
6.
Over a twenty-four-hour period, 15% of the vehicles traveling in the study area exceeded 5 miles per hour
over the speed limit and a field survey conducted by the Traffic Engineering Division determined that
25% of the peak hour traffic was cut-through traffic.
7.
Over a twenty-four-hour period more than 800 vehicles were counted traveling through the study area and
there was at least one reported crash in a 5-year period where the police report identified speed as either a
primary or contributing cause of the crash.
8.
Over a twenty-four-hour period more than 800 vehicles were counted traveling through the study area and
a field survey conducted by the Traffic Engineering Division determined that 25% of the peak hour traffic
was cut-through traffic.
9.
A field survey conducted by the Traffic Engineering Division determined that 25% of the peak hour traffic
was cut-through traffic and there was at least one reported crash in 5 years where the police report
identified speed as either a primary or contributing cause of the crash.
4.1 NTMP Thresholds
From the data collected and the crash data provided, Table 15 through Table 21 summarizes the results for each of the
roadways analyzed within the project area. Results indicate the following:
•
Morningside Drive – None of the nine (9) thresholds meet the criteria; therefore, it does not meet the
minimum traffic calming criterion as described in the COA NTMP.
•
Washington Street – Six of the nine (9) thresholds meet the criteria; therefore, it does meet the minimum
traffic calming criterion as described in the COA NTMP.
•
Constitution Avenue – Two of the nine (9) thresholds meet the criteria; therefore, it does meet the minimum
traffic calming criterion as described in the COA NTMP.
•
Mackland Avenue – None of the nine (9) thresholds meets the criteria; therefore, it does not meet the
minimum traffic calming criterion as described in the COA NTMP.
•
Avenida Del Sol – One of the nine (9) thresholds meets the criteria; therefore, it does meet the minimum
traffic calming criterion as described in the COA NTMP.
•
Montclaire Drive – None of the nine (9) thresholds meet the criteria; therefore, it does not meet the minimum
traffic calming criterion as described in the COA NTMP.
•
Solano Drive – None of the nine (9) thresholds meet the criteria; therefore, it does not meet the minimum
traffic calming criterion as described in the COA NTMP.

5.0 Proposed Alternatives
5.1 Pedestrian Crossings
For this alternative, pedestrian crossings were analyzed at the following intersections:
•
Washington Street and  Sunningdale Avenue
•
Morningside Drive and Constitution Avenue
According to the FHWA Safety Effects of Marked Versus Unmarked Crosswalks at Uncontrolled Locations, published in 2005, it
is recommended that the minimum pedestrian volume be 20 pedestrians crossing per peak hour or a minimum of 15
elderly and/or child pedestrians crossing per peak hour. The City of Albuquerque Bicycle and Trail Crossing Guide,
published in 2022, provides recommendations for crossing designs based on roadway ADT, lanes configuration, and
speed limit (shown below in Table 22). Based on Table 22, the intersections should have the following treatments:
•
Washington Street and Sunningdale Avenue (6,000 ADT; two lanes; 30 mph)
o
There were 31 pedestrians and two (2) bicyclists counted on the north and south legs during the AM
peak hour.
o
Crosswalk marking and signage along the north and south legs. Complementary treatments are also
recommended and can include stop or yield lines, in-street crossing sign, and a raised crosswalk, see
Figure 21 for proposed layout.
•
Morningside Drive and Constitution Avenue (4,578 ADT; two lanes, 30 mph)
o
There were 21 pedestrians and five (5) bicyclists on the east and west legs during the PM peak hour.
o
Crosswalk marking and signage along the east and west legs. Complementary treatments are also
recommended and can include stop or yield lines, in-street crossing sign and raised crosswalk, see
Figure 22 for proposed layout.
Table 22: Crossing Treatment Selection Matrix

McDuffie/Twin Parks Neighborhood
Traffic Calming Study

Figure 22: Morningside Drive and Constitution Avenue

5.2 Striping
This alternative takes into consideration changes in the existing lane configuration by re-striping the lanes to incorporate
bike lanes and/or shift the roadway centerline. Some of the benefits for re-striping an existing roadway are it may slow
traffic down, it is low cost to install and modify, and it does not slowdown emergency vehicles. As shown in Figure 21
through Figure 23, these changes are proposed at the following intersections:
•
Washington Street and Sunningdale Avenue – A change in the existing lane roadway configuration to include
bike lanes along Washington Street on both sides. The existing left-turn bays for northbound and southbound
would be removed to shorten the pedestrian crossing distance, as shown in Figure 21.
•
Washington Street and Constitution Avenue – A change in the existing lane roadway configuration for the
north leg of the intersection to relocate the existing on-street parking, shifting it from the east side to the west
side. This would allow the centerline to shift east to reduce the offset roadway alignment connecting to the
south leg, see Figure 23.
•
Washington Street between Carlisle Boulevard and Morningside Drive – Addition of a striped buffered bike
lane should be considered to narrow the driving lanes to 11-ft.


Figure 23: Washington Street and Constitution Avenue
5.3 Traffic Circle
Traffic circles are a raised island creating a circular intersection, tend to reduce the vehicles traveling speeds, reduce the
number of conflict points, improve safety, and increase access to main street from side street. For this alternative, traffic
circles were evaluated at the following intersections:
•
Montclaire Drive and Mackland Avenue, see Figure 24
•
Avenida Del Sol and Avenida La Resolana, see Figure 25

PDF PAGE 39
McDuffie/Twin Parks Neighborhood
Traffic Calming Study

5.4 Neckdowns and/or Bulbouts
Neckdowns consist of narrowing the roadway at an intersection to slow vehicles down and increase the safety of
pedestrians by reducing their crossing distances. Bulbouts are similar to neckdowns, whereas the existing sidewalk or
curb line is extended into the roadway centerline to shorten the pedestrian crossing distances. For this alternative,
neckdowns and/or bulbouts were examined at the intersections of:
•
Washington Street and Sunningdale Avenue – A bulbout is proposed at the southwest and northeast corners of
the intersection to shorten the pedestrian crossing distances, see Figure 21.
•
Morningside Drive and Constitution Avenue - A bulbout is proposed at the southeast corner of the
intersection to shorten the pedestrian crossing distance and improve sight distances for the northbound
approach, see Figure 23.
•
Avenida Del Sol and Morningside Drive – Bulbouts are proposed to change Avenida Del Sol to a one-way
roadway in the northbound direction, see Figure 26. This would help improve operations at the existing Y-
intersection and reduce traffic volumes on Avenida Del Sol.



Figure 26: Avenida Del Sol and Morningside Drive – Proposed Bulbout



PDF PAGE 41
McDuffie/Twin Parks Neighborhood
Traffic Calming Study



City of Albuquerque                                                 Wilson & Company, Inc., Engineers & Architects   33

5.5 Speed Humps
Speed humps help reduce vehicles speeds, do not restrict parking,  and discourages cut-through traffic. However, speed
humps can only be placed on non-emergency routes, such as Avenida Del Sol. Speed humps were evaluated at Avenida
Del Sol between Morningside Drive and Washington Street, as shown in Figure 27.

Figure 27: Avenida Del Sol – Proposed Speed Humps
6.0 Public Involvement
6.1 Public Meeting #1
The first public meeting to present the existing conditions and potential alternatives was held virtually on February 7,
2023, from 6:00 PM to 7:00 PM. The purpose of the meeting was to present the project and potential alternatives to the
community and stakeholders. The meeting was held virtually via Zoom platform. The meeting consisted of a
PowerPoint presentation by the project team, followed by a question-and-answer session.
Approximately 34 members attended the meeting. Questions from attendees covered topics such as pedestrian and
bicyclist safety, speeding, and feedback on the potential alternatives that were presented.  The full public meeting
summary can be found in Appendix C.
6.1.1 Post Meeting Comments
Public comments were accepted via email for two weeks after the February 7th meeting. There were 48 emails received.
Comments covered topics such as feedback on potential alternatives and additional requests. The full comments can be
found in Appendix C.



PDF PAGE 42
McDuffie/Twin Parks Neighborhood
Traffic Calming Study



City of Albuquerque                                                 Wilson & Company, Inc., Engineers & Architects   34

6.2 Public Meeting #2
The second public meeting to present the alternatives was held virtually on September 7, 2023, from 6:00 PM to 7:00
PM. The purpose of the meeting was to present the project and alternatives to the community and stakeholders. The
meeting was held virtually via Zoom platform. The meeting consisted of a PowerPoint presentation by the project team,
followed by a question-and-answer session.
Approximately 38 members attended the meeting. Questions from attendees covered topics such as pedestrian and
bicyclist safety, speeding, and feedback on the alternatives that were presented.  The full public meeting summary can be
found in Appendix C.
6.2.1 Post Meeting Comments
Public comments were accepted via email for two weeks after the September 7th meeting. There were 60 emails received.
Majority of emails received were for support of the alternatives that were presented. There were two (2) emails that were
opposed to the traffic circle at Mackland and Montclaire. The full comments can be found in Appendix C.
7.0 Summary of Findings and
Recommendations
An assessment was conducted in the McDuffie-Twin Parks neighborhoods, along Washington Street, Constitution
Avenue, Morningside Drive, Avenida Del Sol, Montclaire Drive, Mackland Avenue, and Solano Drive. These roadways
were analyzed to determine if they meet the traffic calming criterion described in the City of Albuquerque (COA)
Neighborhood Traffic Management Program (NTMP), October 2020 results can be found in Table 23.
Table 23: NTMP Criterion Results

NTMP Thresholds

From the results shown in Table 23, only three of the roadways analyzed meet the minimum criterion needed to qualify
for traffic calming. Therefore, different alternatives were analyzed along these roadways which include pedestrian
crossings, striping, traffic circles, neckdowns/bulbouts, and speed bumps. The recommendations for each alternative
analyzed are presented below, conceptual cost estimates are shown in the parentheses:
•
Washington Street and Sunningdale Avenue ($195,972)
o
Pedestrian crossing. Crosswalk marking and signage along the north and south legs.
Complementary treatments are also recommended and can include stop or yield lines, in-street
crossing sign, and a raised crosswalk.
o
Striping. A change in the existing lane roadway configuration to include bike lanes along Washington
Street on both sides. The existing left-turn bays for northbound and southbound would be removed
to shorten the pedestrian crossing distance. Add delineators between the bike lane and travel lane near
intersection/pedestrian crossing.

PDF PAGE 43
McDuffie/Twin Parks Neighborhood
Traffic Calming Study



City of Albuquerque                                                 Wilson & Company, Inc., Engineers & Architects   35

o
Neckdown/bulbout. A bulbout is proposed at the southwest corner of the intersection to shorten
the pedestrian crossing distances. A bulbout is proposed at the northeast corner of the intersection,
on Sunningdale Avenue, in order to make the approach perpendicular to Washington Street.
•
Morningside Drive and Constitution Avenue ($139,913)
o
Pedestrian crossing. Crosswalk marking and signage along the east and west legs. Complementary
treatments are also recommended and can include stop or yield lines, in-street crossing sign and raised
crosswalk. Add delineators between the bike lane and travel lane near intersection/pedestrian
crossing.
o
Neckdown/bulbout. A bulbout is proposed at the southeast corner of the intersection to shorten
the pedestrian crossing distance and improve sight distances for the northbound approach.
•
Washington Street and Constitution Avenue ($58,614)
o
Neckdown/bulbout. A bulbout is proposed at the southwest corner of the intersection to shorten
the pedestrian crossing distance and improve sight distances for the northbound approach. Add
delineators between the bike lane and travel lane near intersection/pedestrian crossing.
•
Constitution Avenue between Carlisle Boulevard and Morningside Drive
o
Striping. Addition of a striped buffered bike lane should be considered to narrow the driving lanes to
11-ft.
•
Montclaire Drive and Mackland Avenue ($66,919)
o
Traffic circle. A change in the existing intersection control type from a two-way stop-controlled
along Montclaire Drive into a traffic circle with yield signs on all approaches.
•
Avenida Del Sol ($35,475)
o
Speed humps/cushions. Addition of speed humps or speed cushions along Avenida Del Sol
between Morningside Drive and Washington Street.
•
Hidden Park
o
Striping. Addition of no parking pavement markings at all access points to Hidden Park. Striping
would include striped chevron markings and “NO PARKING” pavement markings.

PDF PAGE 259
McDuffie/Twin Parks Neighborhood
Traffic Calming Study



City of Albuquerque                                                 Wilson & Company, Inc., Engineers & Architects   C

Appendix C – Public Meeting Summary




PDF PAGE 260
Meeting Summary
1

Meeting Title: McDuffie-Twin Parks Traffic Calming Study – Public Meeting #1
Date:  2/7/2023
Time: 6:00 pm – 7:00 pm
Location: Zoom – City of Albuquerque
Attendees:
See page 3. There were 34 attendees, including City of Albuquerque staff and Wilson & Company
staff who attended as Panelists.

I.
Introduction and Presentation
Audra Gallegos opened the meeting. She then introduced Councilor Tammy Fiebelkorn on the call.
The councilor then described the safety issues within the McDuffie-Twin Parks area and expressed
hope that this study would provide solutions to those issues.
Audra Gallegos (Wilson & Company) then began a slideshow presentation.
II.
Q&A Session
A question & answer session followed the presentation. The questions and responses are listed below:
Participant Question
Project Team Response
Question, via audio: A member of the
Neighborhood Association Board (NAB) had
questions related to Solano Drive (between
Lomas Boulevard and Mackland Avenue). She
mentioned that she has walked the area with
Laura Rummler (COA) to identify locations
that need traffic calming initiatives
implemented and Solano Drive was one of
them. Is there a plan for Solano Drive?
Audra: We are planning to have striping
recommendations for no parking near the entrances
of the park, but let me get with Laura and the rest of
the City of Albuquerque Staff and we will get back to
you on traffic calming along Solano.
Question, via audio: What is the plan for the
pedestrian crosswalk at Mackland Avenue
crossing Carlisle Boulevard?
Councilor: The crosswalk has been painted as well
as an order has been placed to install a pedestrian
rectangular flashing beacons to alert drivers of
pedestrians crossing. There is no date set in place at
the moment but as soon as the beacons are
delivered, they will be placed.
Question, via audio: There is a high number
of bicyclists along Constitution Avenue
crossing Morningside Drive. The bulbout
presented at that intersection seemed to reduce
the bike lane for bicyclist.
Audra: The intersection example does show a
bulbout and provides a bike ramp for bicyclist to be
able to cross the intersection and then it ties back to
the existing bike lane. This is the same for other
bulbout examples shown.
Comment, via audio: I wanted to clarify that I
am really excited that you are going to put
something at Mackland Avenue and Montclaire
Drive because it is such a weird intersection.
People do slow down, but there is so many
sight problems with bushes and stuff. It is very
hard to cross there.

I think most of the wide intersections that we
have in the area could use something like that.
Audra: Thank you, we appreciate your comment.

PDF PAGE 261
McDuffie-Twin Parks Traffic Calming Study – Public Meeting #1
2/7/2023
pg. 2


Participant Question
Project Team Response
Comment, via audio: I am kind of opposed to
the Avenida del Sol example making it one-way.
Audra: That is why we are here. We want to hear
from all of you and get your input. And if you do not
like something, just let us know.
Comment, via Audio: I wanted to echo this
idea of some sort of traffic calming at Solano
Drive and Mackland Avenue. We were almost
run down one time.
Audra: Thank you, we appreciate your comment.
Comment, via audio: Carlisle Boulevard has
become more dangerous in my view. I have
seen pedestrians actually running and diving at
the intersection north of Constitution Avenue
where the streets have stripping and flashing
lights. That is a difficult street right now.
Audra: Thank you, we appreciate your comments.
Question, via audio: Do you take out the on-
street parking at Washington Street just north
of Constitution Avenue in your proposed
example?
Audra: The example does show the on-street
parking switching to the west side of the roadway,
currently placed on the east side. These are just
examples. We want to have the conversations with
you all and what you want to see and do not see out
there. However, to shift the on-street parking on this
west side instead. What this does is, it shifts that
southbound through lane over so that if somebody is
traveling southbound on Washington Street the
offset is not as strong.
Question, via audio: Removing the on-street
parking of the west side, would become a
problem for the people that live on the east side
that currently parked at the roadway. Vehicles
parking at the other side of the roadway would
be somewhat treacherous and difficult for
current users to walk across the traffic along
Washington Street.
Audra: Thank you, we appreciate your comment.
Comment, via audio: A lot of participants
have suggested a traffic circle at the intersection
of Washington Street and Constitution Avenue.
However, about 15 years ago the City of
Albuquerque try to put a traffic circle but it was
not big enough and it did not work.
Audra: Yes, that is correct. There is not enough
space for a traffic circle to fit.
Question, via chat: Washington Street and
Hannett Avenue is a popular crossing for
families to walk to the park. Please consider
how to make this crossing safer and thank you
for including it in the study!
Audra: For that intersection, adding bulbouts will
reduced the pedestrian crossing distance. Advanced
signing and stripping would also be placed to help
make that pedestrian crossing more visible.
Question, via chat: Have you obtained
information on how many times the wall at the
southwest corner of Washington and
Constitution has been knocked down over the
last 10 years?
Audra: We recieved the crash data from the 2016 to
2020 data, but we do not have the crash data at this
time on how many times it has been hit. However, in
the example provided adding a bulbout will move
over the vehicular traffic away from that location and
it would also help slow down vehicles that are
turning.
Question, via chat: Has Councilor secured or
approved funding for this project?
Audra: As part of this project, we will look at
conceptual costs for these examples that will help

PDF PAGE 262
McDuffie-Twin Parks Traffic Calming Study – Public Meeting #1
2/7/2023
pg. 3


Participant Question
Project Team Response
Councilor look at what type of funding would be
needed for these projects.
Councilor: Our district did set aside money to pay
for this study so we can come up with some
alternatives. If the community wants the different
alternatives, then will move forward. The next step is
to provide a cost estimate of the alternatives selected
moving forward, and then I will find funding for it as
well. But just know this is a priority for me.
Question, via chat: Is 48 hours a typical
amount of time to collect vehicle data?
Audra: Yes, 48 hours is the typical time period for
collecting tube count data.
Question, via chat: The crash data showed no
incidences on Morningside Drive between
Hannett Avenue and Indian School Road, and
we see cars speeding there frequently was the
period of time of the study sufficient to get the
true picture?
Audra: The crash data that we got provides
information related to reported crashes that had a
police report done. Therefore, if those incidents
occurred outside the time period or do not have a
crash report, we would not be able to incorporate
that information.
Question, via chat: I noticed that based on the
crash study visual (heat map) shared, there were
quite a few crash spots directly surrounding
Altura Park (especially as compared to Twin
Parks) but Hannett Avenue and Aspen Avenue
were not included in the study. Can these two
roads be added to the study?
Audra: I think that is something we can talk about
internally, and then we can provide an answer back
for that one.
Question, via chat: What about speed bumps
in more areas to look slow traffic like Avenida
del Sol?
Audra: Speed bumps are definitely an option.
Although, speed bumps cannot be placed on
emergency routes. Speed bumps are definitely an
option if that is what the neighborhood wants.
Question, via chat: Will traffic calming on
some streets cause traffic to shift over to other
areas that don not have traffic calming devices?
Audra: This is always a possibility. Traffic calming
can help reduce cut through traffic in one
neighborhood and detour the traffic onto other
streets.
Comment, via chat: We get so much cut
through traffic on Montclaire Drive (Lomas
Boulevard to Carlisle Boulevard), and the stop
signs at Montclaire and Mackland are frequently
ignored.
Audra: If the stop signs are being ignored on
Montclaire Drive and Mackland Avenue, a traffic
circle would really benefit since there will be a
physical barrier in the middle of the intersection.
Drivers entering the intersection will need to slow
down.
Comments, via chat: What about adding
flashing lights to the stop signs at Washington
Street and Constitution Avenue? This is an
extremely dangerous intersection.
Audra: Yes, that is a possibility of adding flashing
stop signs to that intersection.
Comment, via chat: I like the idea of moving
the southbound lane on Washington Street at
Constitution Avenue to move it to the east. Can
you clarify if any of the street parking on
Washington Street, north of Hannett Avenue
will change?
Audra: The only area that we had looked at to
potentially change the parking was at this one block
of Washington Street, just north of Constitution
Avenue, just to shift that lane over. The other
parking areas that we looked at restriping did not
affect the existing on street parking.
Comment, via chat: There are large
communication boxes at the intersection of
Washington and Constitution and one that is
Audra: Thank you, we appreciate your comment.

PDF PAGE 263
McDuffie-Twin Parks Traffic Calming Study – Public Meeting #1
2/7/2023
pg. 4


Participant Question
Project Team Response
underground that is accessed by a manhole and
it is used regularly.
Comment, via chat: The lights on Carlisle
Boulevard, do they stop traffic?
Audra: This intersection will have rapid flashing
beacons that are triggered when someone pushes the
button. The lights then flash to alert drivers that a
pedestrian is crossing but they do not stop the
traffic.
Question, via chat: Is it possible to get a copy
of the PowerPoint presentation and the data
presented?
Audra: The PowerPoint will be provided in a PDF
to the city and the city will post it in their website.
Also, once the report is finalized that will also be
posted on the City’s website.
Comment, via chat: It is hard to see traffic
coming west on Lomas because of a utility pole,
is there a plan to address it?
Audra: That intersection was not part of the scope
of this project, but we can talk internally with the city
to see if there are any plans for that intersection in
the future.
Comment, via chat: There is a large number
of pedestrians crossing at Sunningdale and
Washington, I just wanted to make sure that it
got included in this conversation.
Audra: Yes, we did show an example of a pedestrian
mid-block crossing that would allow pedestrian to
cross there.
Question, via audio: Can you remind us of
what the next steps are? What happens next?
Audra: We will take the input provided in during
this presentation and incorporate the information
into the report. Based on the inputs we have received
we would determine what improvement would be
moving forward and then once we have preliminary
recommendations set we will be scheduling another
public meeting.
Question, via audio: Will there be a way to get
a copy of the report before the public hearing?
Audra: Yes, there is a possibility of placing the draft
report onto the city’s website prior to the next
meeting and notify the meeting attendees when they
have been uploaded.

III.
Closing and Schedule
Audra: If any additional questions or comments please email them to me. (Audra put the
contact info back on the screen.) The presentation from tonight will be placed on the project
webpage. The meeting concluded.

IV.
Summary of Comments Received after the Public Meeting
Public comments were accepted for a two-week period after the public meeting. A total of 48 emails
were received. The following is a summary of the comments received:
•
Out of the 48 emails received, majority of commenters were for the traffic circle at Montclair
and Mackland and installing the proposed bulbouts.
•
Fifteen (15) emails received were specifically for the pedestrian crossings.
•
Nine (9) emails received were for speed humps being placed on various streets and two (2)
commenters were opposed to any speed humps.
•
Five (5) commenters were opposed to the traffic circle.
•
Twelve (12) comments received were opposed to the one-way on Avenida del Sol.
•
Additional requests received were as follows:
o Traffic calming on Solano, Aspen, Altura Avenue, and Hannett
o Traffic circle at Morningside and Avenida de Resolana
o Traffic circle at Washington Street and Hannett-Sunningdale

PDF PAGE 264
McDuffie-Twin Parks Traffic Calming Study – Public Meeting #1
2/7/2023
pg. 5


o Switch stop signs on Morningside and Avenida de Resolana
o Stop sign at Morningside and Avenida de Resolana
o Stop sign on Avenida del Sol and Morningside
o Stop sign at Mackland and Montclair
o Crosswalk across Mackland at entrance to park
o Stop ahead markings at Constitution Avenue and Washington Street
o Flashing stop signs on Constitution Avenue and Washington Street
o More police enforcement
o Multiway stops or traffic signals
o Speed radar signs
o Speed cameras
o Reflective signing on wall at Washington Street
o Metal posts to protect wall on Washington Street

PDF PAGE 275
•
City of Albuquerque Neighborhood Traffic Management Program Policy Manual
•
Nine (9) thresholds, one (1) or more must meet
1.
Over a twenty-four-hour period, 15% of the vehicles traveling in the study area exceeded 7 miles per hour over the speed limit.
2.
Three reported crashes in a 5-year period where the police report identified speed as either a primary of contributing cause of
the crash.
3.
A crash involving a pedestrian or cyclist in a school zone in a five-year period.
4.
Over a twenty-four-hour period more than 800 vehicles were counted traveling through the study area and 15% of the vehicles
traveling in the study area exceeded 5 miles per hour over the speed limit.
5.
Over a twenty-four-hour period, 15% of the vehicles traveling in the study area exceeded 5 miles per hour over the speed limit
and there was at least one reported crash in a 5-year period where the police report identified speed as either a primary or
contributing cause of the crash.
6.
Over a twenty-four-hour period, 15% of the vehicles traveling in the study area exceeded 5 miles per hour over the speed limit
and a field survey conducted by the Traffic Engineering Division determined that 25% of the peak hour traffic was cut-through
traffic.
7.
Over a twenty-four-hour period more than 800 vehicles were counted traveling through the study area and there was at least
one reported crash in a 5-year period where the police report identified speed as either a primary or contributing cause of the
crash.
8.
Over a twenty-four-hour period more than 800 vehicles were counted traveling through the study area and a field survey
conducted by the Traffic Engineering Division determined that 25% of the peak hour traffic was cut-through traffic.
9.
A field survey conducted by the Traffic Engineering Division determined that 25% of the peak hour traffic was cut-through traffic
and there was at least one reported crash in 5 years where the police report identified speed as either a primary or contributing
cause of the crash.

• Low cost to install and modify
• Does not inhibit emergency vehicles
Cons
• Maintenance
• Effectiveness may be low
Striping
McDuffie-Twin Parks
Traffic Calming Study

PDF PAGE 279
Benefits
• Improves safety
• Lower vehicle speeds
• Reduce conflict points
• Increased access to main street from
side street
Cons
• Slows down emergency vehicles
• May eliminate on-street parking
• May require modifications to curb,
gutter, and sidewalks

distances

• May slow right turning emergency
vehicles

PDF PAGE 292
Meeting Summary
1
.

Meeting Title:  McDuffie-Twin Parks Traffic Calming Study – Public Meeting #2
Date:  9/7/2023
Time: 6:00 pm – 7:00 pm
Location: Zoom – City of Albuquerque
Attendees: See page 4. There were 38 attendees, including City of Albuquerque staff and Wilson &
Company staff who attended as Panelists.

I.
Introduction and Presentation
Audra Gallegos opened the meeting. She then introduced Councilor Tammy Fiebelkorn on the call. The
Councilor then described the safety issues within the McDuffie-Twin Parks area and that the meeting
would describe the findings of the study.
Audra Gallegos (Wilson & Company) then began a slideshow presentation.
II.
Q&A Session
A question & answer session followed the presentation. The questions and responses are listed below:
Participant Question
Project Team Response
Question, via audio: I had a question about
the roundabouts. People are basically whipping
around the roundabout and there is concern
that the cars would then just end up crashing
into the houses. Is there going to be any sort of
speed calming related to the roundabouts to
help prevent those concerns or like bollards
along the sides?
Audra: Entering the traffic circle people will slow
down due to yield signs, and while entering through
them. Then stripping may be included at the
approaches to help navigate people through it.
Question, via audio: Crossing the intersection
at Mackland and Carlisle that is, I think, the
most risk I take every day compared to
everything else I do. Was there any discussion
or thoughts about how to make it safer?
Councilor: Pedestrian crossing signs have been
installed and rapid flashing beacons are expected to
be install relatively soon.
Question, via audio: Could someone reiterate
what was just said about the Montclaire and
Mackland intersection?
Audra: An elongated traffic circle is proposed at this
intersection. Drivers would have to slow down
entering the intersection and then make their way
around the traffic circle in order to make their
turning movements.
Question, via audio: When I look at the data
that was presented, there was not much data to
warrant a traffic calming technique. As extreme
as that elongated traffic circle, can you
enlighten me in the thinking behind this
(Montclaire and Mackland intersection)?
Audra: At this intersection, we looked at sight
distances since it is not a conventional four-legged
intersection. Therefore, an elongated traffic circle
would help. This would help slow people down
entering the intersection, and they would have to go
around the traffic circle.
Question, via audio: Are the lanes full size
lanes at the traffic circles for big vehicles to
make turns? Big vehicles might not be able to
Audra: In the design process, we will model vehicle
turning templates based on different vehicles.

PDF PAGE 293
McDuffie-Twin Parks Traffic Calming Study – Public Meeting #2

9/7/2023
pg. 2

Participant Question
Project Team Response
navigate through the traffic circle (Montclaire
and Mackland intersection).
Comment, via audio: If you want to slow
people down, move the stop sign on Solano at
Mackland to Montclaire.
Audra: Thank you, we can look at the volumes at
this intersection and see if moving the stop sign
would be warranted.
Comment, via audio: When approaching
pedestrian islands, it seems like drivers feel like
it is a bottleneck. And what winds up
happening is that drivers wind up merging into
the bike lane.

Maybe adding delineators would prevent drivers
from winding up merging into the bike lanes.

Tim Brown: We will look into adding delineators or
some kind of separator. I appreciate that comment
very much.
Comment, via audio: I guess delineators
would also be needed at the Constitution and
Washington intersection. I am assuming from
the pictures shown that there is a bulb out for
cyclist to ride through it.

Cyclist can ride through that. OK, yeah, this is
looking amazing. Like I said, I ride down here
five days a week. I use Constitution and I see
people crossing the street all the time. They are
waiting there with their dogs ready to walk to
the park. So, this is really good stuff. I
appreciate the work here.
Audra: Yes. So, there would essentially be a curb cut
through it and the bicyclist could ride through it.




Thank you so much.

Question, via chat: Please confirm which park
would have the no parking at the entrances?
Audra: That is the McDuffie Hidden Park. No
parking signs would be placed at the various
entrances around the park.
Comment, via chat: In other cities, I have
seen parking curbs used as protected bike lanes,
maybe a potential escalation if the flexible
delineators do not work well.


Comment, via audio: I really want to strongly
emphasize to the city that they have to do
something about speed limits.
Councilor: Thank you. I do think that is one of the
main reasons that we are doing this study and
looking for alternatives to do traffic calming in these
areas because residents are walking to that park,
biking to work. I know I might not be able to solve
the speeding problem citywide. But you know, these
kinds of interventions can calm traffic down and that
is the whole hope here. But I definitely hear your
frustration and I share it.
Question, via audio: Is there any plans for
adding mid-block traffic calming, like speed
bumps or other things like that?
Audra: Speed bumps cannot be placed on
emergency routes such as arterials which limits where
they can be placed. Adding some improvements
along Washington at Hannah and Sunningdale
hopefully will help reduce speeding.
Question, via chat: For the segment of
Washington between Hannett and Constitution,
Audra: We decided not to change the parking lane at
this particular intersection.

Participant Question

III.
Closing and Schedule
Audra: If any additional questions or comments please email them to me. (Audra put the contact
info back on the screen.) The presentation from tonight will be placed on the project webpage.
The meeting concluded.

IV.
Summary of Comments Received after the Public Meeting
Public comments were accepted for a two-week period after the public meeting. A total of 60 emails were
received. The following is a summary of the comments received:
•
Out of the 60 emails, twenty-seven (27) emails were in support of all six traffic calming measures
proposed.
•
Few of the proposed traffic calming measures got additional support such as:
o For – mid-block crossing, bulbouts, and stripping at Washington and Hannett/Sunningdale
(36 emails)
o For – stripping and bulbouts at Constitution and Washington (32 emails)
o For – mid-block crossing, bulbouts, and center island at Constitution and Morningside (32
emails)
o For – speed bumps along Avenida Del Sol (29 emails)
•
Two (2) commenters were opposed to the traffic circle at Mackland and Montclaire.
o Additional comments received were as follows:

Adding delineators at the intersections with the proposed bulbouts

Conduct a speed study along Indian School near the intersection with Washington,
look at speed bumps along Indian School

Conduct a speed study along Washington near the intersection with Indian School,
look at speed bumps for northbound approach

Nine (9) emails related to drivers ignoring the stop sign at Aspen and Hannett

Six (6) emails mentioned drivers speeding through Altura Park Neighborhood, using it
as a cut-through traffic between Carlisle and Washington potently conducting a speed
study in this area

Look at the possibility of adding speed cushions on Washington and Constitution

Adding stripped crosswalks at entrances to McDuffie Park

An additional assessment of sidewalk condition and medians to be conducted within
the study area

Limited visibility for pedestrians crossing Constitution and Morningside

Two (2) emails mentioned conducting a safety study at Hannett and Aspen

City of Albuquerque Neighborhood Traffic Management Program Policy Manual
•
Nine (9) thresholds, one (1) or more must meet
1.
Over a twenty-four-hour period, 15% of the vehicles traveling in the study area exceeded 7 miles per hour over the speed limit.
2.
Three reported crashes in a 5-year period where the police report identified speed as either a primary of contributing cause of
the crash.
3.
A crash involving a pedestrian or cyclist in a school zone in a five-year period.
4.
Over a twenty-four-hour period more than 800 vehicles were counted traveling through the study area and 15% of the vehicles
traveling in the study area exceeded 5 miles per hour over the speed limit.
5.
Over a twenty-four-hour period, 15% of the vehicles traveling in the study area exceeded 5 miles per hour over the speed limit
and there was at least one reported crash in a 5-year period where the police report identified speed as either a primary or
contributing cause of the crash.
6.
Over a twenty-four-hour period, 15% of the vehicles traveling in the study area exceeded 5 miles per hour over the speed limit
and a field survey conducted by the Traffic Engineering Division determined that 25% of the peak hour traffic was cut-through
traffic.
7.
Over a twenty-four-hour period more than 800 vehicles were counted traveling through the study area and there was at least
one reported crash in a 5-year period where the police report identified speed as either a primary or contributing cause of the
crash.
8.
Over a twenty-four-hour period more than 800 vehicles were counted traveling through the study area and a field survey
conducted by the Traffic Engineering Division determined that 25% of the peak hour traffic was cut-through traffic.
9.
A field survey conducted by the Traffic Engineering Division determined that 25% of the peak hour traffic was cut-through traffic
and there was at least one reported crash in 5 years where the police report identified speed as either a primary or contributing
cause of the crash.
